WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
As was universally expected, the RBA Monetary Policy
Board (MPB) lifted the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6% at its
September 2026 meeting.
This is quite the turnaround from the 11 August meeting,
where an extended period on hold looked to be the base case.

At that time, the data had broken against the RBA’s more
hawkish view, and its published forecasts did not support
further hikes. But the MPB can now point to the emergence of
some of the upside risks to inflation it warned of in August.
The post-meeting statement was notable for its emphasis
on the Middle East conflict, noting that “global energy prices
are now much higher than had been assumed in the August
forecasts” – “much higher” is stronger language than one

normally sees in an RBA statement. The RBA’s liaison indicates
that firms are passing this on or planning to do so. The pass-
through was characterised as partial, and additional to
domestically driven inflation arising from capacity pressures. We
read this as implying that the RBA is concerned about upside
risks to underlying inflation in Q4 of this year, as well as Q3.
The RBA is also wary about pressure on both construction
costs and retail prices of IT goods arising from the AI/data

centre boom, although in the media conference, the Governor
clarified that risks there were building rather than materialising.
The broader background highlighted in the Governor’s opening
statement to the media conference, is of demand outstripping
supply. The statement evidenced this by noting the stronger
July CPI and June quarter GDP results than expected.
The accompanying statement was notable for the absence of
language about the labour market being a little tight, despite

this being the inter-meeting message from the Governor
and other staff (see, for example, the Governor’s opening
statement on 18 September) and the language in the post-
meeting media conference. Views clearly differ on this: we
note that the Treasury forecasts underlying the May budget
and the recent Intergenerational Review imply both more
spare capacity in the labour market (a lower NAIRU) and faster
growth in potential from wherever the starting point is (higher

productivity growth and trend increase in participation).
Instead, the post-meeting statement characterised the
labour market as easing “broadly as expected”, despite the
unemployment rate already rising higher than the August SMP
forecast of 4.5% for the December quarter.
RBA hikes in September, November follow-up now expected
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
The decision was unanimous, suggesting that any qualms
external MPB members might have had about the RBA’s

downbeat view of supply capacity were overruled by resurgent
oil prices and the stronger than forecast CPI and GDP data.
With upside risks being seen to have emerged, the post-meeting
statement language included that the Board “will continue to
do what it considers necessary to bring inflation sustainably
back to target, including increasing the cash rate target further if
needed”, a small but meaningful change from the “if upside risks
materialise” language from the previous meeting, and back to

the tone of the language at the June meeting.
The bar for a follow-up hike in November is low. Indeed,
judging by the post-meeting rhetoric, a November hike is now
the base case, absent a lasting resolution of the Middle East
conflict beforehand, or some other event that significantly
lowers the outlook for energy-related costs in Australia. The
bar for hikes beyond that is much higher, given the cumulative
rise in interest rates, and noting that the labour market is

easing and the housing market will likely weaken further.
The August inflation data released the day after the meeting
was in line with our expectations. We therefore continue to
expect Q3 trimmed mean inflation around 0.9%qtr, too high for
the RBA’s comfort, and upside risks for Q4. Likewise, there are
no implications for our rates view from this week’s inflation data.
Forecast changes
• We have updated our financial forecasts to reflect the change

in our rate view.
• Our labour market forecasts are unchanged on the first
decimal place, but there is some modest upside risk to our
unemployment forecast.
• Our updated GDP profile can be found on page 9.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: searching for signal
led the correction to date, the fact that the other capitals
are also reporting price declines clearly indicates that the
correction is deepening and broadening, as higher interest
rates collide with the tax policy changes announced in May’s

budget. Sentiment effects indeed remain at play, as evinced
by the continued pull-back in investor credit growth . Westpac
anticipates a peak-to-trough decline of around 7%, with tight
on-market supply limiting the extent of the correction. For
more, see our latest Housing Pulse.
Offshore, there was plenty of data to dissect.
In the US, inflation eased in August despite ongoing strength
in household demand and higher energy prices. Headline PCE
inflation rose 0.3%mth and 3.4%yr, while core PCE increased

0.2%mth and 3.0%yr. Encouragingly for the FOMC, the
share of PCE components rising by more than 3% over the
past year, a measure frequently cited by Fed Chairman Kevin
Warsh, declined to 45% from 49%, suggesting some easing in
broad-based inflation pressures. At the same time, household
spending remained robust, increasing 0.9%mth, equivalent
to around 0.6%mth in real terms. That said, personal income
growth was soft, rising 0.2% in the month, signalling

households opted to draw down savings.
The ISM manufacturing survey was stronger than the
headline suggested. While the index edged down 0.1pt to
54.5, manufacturing has now expanded for nine consecutive
months. There were some signs of capacity constraints
emerging, with production softened in the month but backlogs
up 4.6pts to 56.4, suggesting that firms struggled to keep
pace with demand. New orders rose 1.6pts to 55.3, primarily
due to domestic demand as new export orders eased, backlogs

increased. Firms responded by lifting employment, 1.5pts to
52.7. Against this backdrop manufacturers saw a renewed
acceleration in cost pressures, with the prices paid index
jumping 6.8pts to 77.9. Firms cited higher steel and aluminium
prices, elevated energy costs, rising freight charges and
ongoing semiconductor shortages, with every major industry
reporting higher input costs during the month.
The past week’s data will have done nothing to bridge the

clear divide across the FOMC, as evident in this week’s busy
Fedspeak. Some participants argued for further rate hikes,
citing strong inflation, diminished supply capacity and policy
that is not yet restrictive. Others counselled patience, pointing
to progress on inflation and that September’s hike buys time
to assess upcoming data. The one thing both agree on is that
inflation will be the primary determinant of the timing and
extent of further rate hikes. Our view remains that one further

hike is likely this cycle to bring policy closer to neutral.
In Australia, this week’s main event was the RBA Monetary
Policy Board’s (MPB) decision to raise the cash rate by 25bps
to 4.60%. This outcome was universally expected given
Governor Bullock had already communicated that upside risks
to inflation had materialised – the conditionality established
by the MPB back in August for considering another rate hike.
Governor Bullock made explicit note of three upside risks.

The Middle East conflict was emphasised as the risk that had
“materialised”, given the re-escalation and renewed surge in crude
oil and refined product prices over recent months. The RBA’s
liaison warns this could lead to further pass-through of costs
and more persistent inflation pressures. On top of this, domestic
capacity pressures and the data centre/AI investment boom were
also noted as upside risks that the MPB remain alert to.
In a video update mid-week, Chief Economist Luci Ellis

explains why the bar for a follow-up rate hike in November is
low. We now expect a 25bp increase in November, provided
there is no sudden resolution of the Middle East conflict
beforehand, or some other event that significantly lowers the
outlook for energy-related costs.
Subsequently, the August CPI revealed that underlying
(trimmed mean) inflation rose 0.2% (3.4%yr), which was
slightly below the market’s expectation but spot on Westpac’s
forecast. As usual, the monthly detail was mixed, but the key

drivers of underlying inflation remain centred on housing-
related components, particularly rents and new dwelling
purchases. Headline inflation remained volatile, with the
unwind of the fuel excise cut causing another spike in fuel
prices. We have maintained our forecast for a 0.9% lift in
trimmed mean inflation in Q3, and with upside risks building
for Q4, a follow-up rate hike in November appears most likely.
Regarding the current growth pulse, household spending

surprised with a flat reading in August. Services spend fell back
following recent strength that was driven by major sporting
events, but this was offset by an increase in goods spend,
particularly in fuel and electric vehicles. Together with our
latest card data for early September, the consumer spending
pulse is shading a touch softer. However, another solid
outcome for business investment could once again be on the
cards for Q3. August’s goods trade data showcased another

significant spike in data centre-related equipment imports, up
$4.3bn, similar in scale to Q1. While clearly import-driven, our
modelling suggests that the local economic impact of such
investment is likely to be around half of the total investment.
Before moving offshore, a final note on housing. Cotality’s
latest update revealed that dwelling prices fell another 1.1%
nationally in September. While Sydney and Melbourne have
Sian Fenner, Head of Business and Industry Economics

Ryan Wells, Economist
Illiana Jain, Economist

### Page 4

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
4
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
In China, policymakers announced another round of targeted
support measures aimed at stabilising growth and supporting
the struggling property sector. The PBOC lowered rates on its
Pledged Supplementary Lending facility by 25bps to 1.5% and
expanded quotas for targeted lending programs. Authorities

also introduced subsidies for eligible first-home buyers. The
subsidy applies to homes with a floor area of 120m² or less
and a value below CNY1.5mn, with support capped at 1ppt of
mortgage costs, up to CNY10,000 and for a maximum term of
five years. The measures complement reforms announced in
August to improve developer financing conditions and support
housing completion. However, weak credit demand and
confidence remains a significant constraint, suggesting policy

transmission is still limited.
The September PMI data pointed to a modest improvement
in activity, although the details were less convincing.
Manufacturing returned to expansionary territory, rising 0.9pts
to 50.1, supported by stronger production and new orders.
Much of the improvement, however, reflected a rebound in
prices, with the raw materials price index rising 7.6pts and
producer prices increasing 6.2pts. Labour market conditions
remained weak, with the employment index falling a further

0.6pts and remaining below 50 for almost four years. Conditions
in the non-manufacturing sector also improved, with the
services PMI lifting from 49.0 to 50.2, driven largely by stronger
construction activity. Input and output prices moved higher,
selling prices returned back above 50, export orders improved
to 48.5 and employment increased 0.5pts, although at 45.9 it
remains firmly in contractionary territory. Overall, the surveys
suggest activity is stabilising and policy support is gaining some

traction, but weak labour demand, subdued credit growth and
soft domestic demand indicate the recovery remains fragile.
In Japan, the September Tankan survey delivered another
constructive signal on the economy and reinforced the case
for further policy normalisation. Business conditions improved
by 2pts for large manufacturers and 5pts more broadly, while
profitability indicators strengthened and capital expenditure
intentions remained elevated. Large manufacturers expect

fixed investment to increase by 11.6% in FY2026, while large
non-manufacturers expect investment growth of 11.2%,
pointing to continued confidence in the medium-term outlook.
Capacity indicators improved further across both
manufacturing and non-manufacturing sectors, with measures
of excess production capacity becoming less negative
and indicating that demand remains sufficiently strong to
keep pressure on existing capacity. Labour shortages also

intensified, with employment conditions becoming more
negative across most industries and firm sizes, pointing to a
progressively tighter labour market. Inflation expectations
remained anchored around 2%, providing further evidence
that Japan is moving towards a more sustainable inflation
environment. Overall, the survey was consistent with an
economy operating close to capacity and supports the view
that a December rate hike remains a realistic possibility.

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Resilient activity and simmering inflation
While bumpy and uneven, New Zealand’s economic
recovery has continued over the past few months, with
activity showing resilience in the face of continued global
headwinds. Under the surface, however, inflation pressures

are bubbling away, with much of that pressure centred on
parts of the economy that aren’t sensitive to interest rates.
Economic activity getting back on firmer footing
Looking first at recent activity data, the economy has been
finding its feet again after taking a knock in the wake of the
Middle East war. That has been seen most clearly in the jobs
market, with the number of filled jobs rising by 0.9% in the
year to August. While not shooting the lights out, that is still a

noticeable improvement from the early part of the year, when
filled job numbers were falling.
Under the surface, it’s still a very mixed picture. Agriculture
remains the main engine of growth, with the strength in
commodity export earnings helping to boost incomes,
confidence and spending in regions with strong rural backbones.
That’s supported firm job growth in regions like Canterbury
(where filled job numbers are up 1.9% over the past year), Otago

(+1.5%) and Southland (+3.1%). In contrast, jobs growth remains
much more moderate in Auckland (up 0.6%) and Wellington
(up just 0.4%). That town-and-country split is likely to remain a
feature of the economic landscape for some time yet.
Looking ahead, businesses expect trading conditions to
remain resilient through the final months of the year. That
was reflected in September’s Business Outlook survey.
While the survey’s gauges of expected business activity and

general business confidence eased over the past month, they
remain at firm levels, consistent with continued moderate
growth through the latter part of the year. That resilience has
also been evident in the household sector, with consumer
confidence largely steady through September.
The resilience in household and business sentiment is notable,
as fuel prices have taken a big step higher over the past month.
The average price of 91 unleaded has risen by around 49c/litre –

albeit late in the month and likely not fully reflected in surveyed
confidence – taking it back to the levels seen during the early
stages of the Middle East conflict. Those higher fuel prices
have taken a bite out of household disposable incomes, with
spending on fuel up about 22% compared with this time last
year, even though the volume of fuel sold is down around 11%.
But even with the lift in fuel prices, we haven’t seen the same
degree of pessimism as earlier in the year, when fuel prices first

spiked. Notably, those higher fuel prices haven’t spilled over
into a broader lift in output price pressures either (at least at
this stage). Inflation expectations were steady in September’s
household and business surveys. Similarly, the number of firms
planning to raise prices fell back despite a lift in wage and cost
expectations. However, while that stability in pricing gauges is
welcome, measures of inflation pressure remain elevated.
Taken together, recent data reinforce indications that the

economy is shaking off the impacts of the Iran war.
Persistent inflation drivers and the RBNZ
Turning to inflation, the spike in oil prices earlier this year saw
annual inflation climb to 4.1% in the year to June. With local
fuel costs rising again in recent weeks, we continue to expect
inflation to remain above 3% until at least mid-2027.
However, even before the recent rise in fuel costs, inflation had
been running hot for an extended period. Excluding the rise

in food and fuel prices, inflation in the year to June was 2.9%,
and it’s been running close to or above 3% for five years now.
As we discuss in our recent report, much of the strength in
inflation over the past year has been related to administered
costs, such as local council rates, or to sectors that are less
exposed to competitive pressures, such as electricity and
insurance. Those increases have persisted for an extended period,
and we expect further sizeable increases in many of these costs.

This ongoing firmness is a key reason why we expect domestic
(non-tradables) inflation to be higher than the RBNZ expects
over the longer term. It’s also part of the reason why we
expect the RBNZ will ultimately need to raise the OCR by
more than it assumed in its September policy statement.
We are forecasting a 25bp rate rise at the RBNZ’s December
meeting and expect the OCR to peak at 4% next year.
This is a tough environment for the RBNZ. With ongoing

increases in administered prices boosting core inflation at a
time when energy prices are driving headline inflation well
above the target range, it’s hard for the RBNZ to look through
them. The RBNZ had little headroom to absorb upside inflation
surprises, even before the recent spike in oil prices.
Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist

### Page 6

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
6
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
But getting inflation back to target in the face of an oil price
spike and high administered prices will not be easy and involves
some tough trade-offs. Administered prices tend to be less
responsive to changes in economic conditions. As a result,
interest rate hikes are likely to have a bigger impact on parts of

the economy that are not the main drivers of inflation. Tighter
policy will also weigh on the labour market and near-term
growth, while increasing households’ debt-servicing costs.
This is an uncomfortable but necessary trade-off. Looking
through, or not responding to, these sorts of large and persistent
cost increases would still leave households facing significant
increases in overall living costs. In the long run, it would be
more damaging for households and the economy if generalised

inflation were to become embedded in price setting behaviour.
The Kiwi has lost its wings
Adding to near-term inflation risks has been the drop in the NZ
dollar. On a trade-weighted basis, the Kiwi has fallen around
4% since the start of September, and it’s begun the December
quarter around 4% below the level the RBNZ assumed in its
September policy statement.
The slide in the Kiwi is mainly due to the strength of the US
dollar, with NZD/USD down nearly 5% since the start of

September to sit just below 0.56 at the time of writing. Moves
against other currencies have been more modest. For example,
NZD/AUD is down 2% over the same period.
Technically, NZD/USD seems oversold. In the short term,
trends in the US dollar will dominate, including any response
to the US labour market report due on Friday evening. Any
turnaround in the USD would likely see NZD/USD trade back
towards the top of the 0.5580–0.5680 range that is governing

the pair for the time being.
NZD/AUD will continue to be driven by changing views on
interest rate differentials between the RBNZ and RBA. This
week, NZD/AUD corrected higher from around 0.8070 to as high
as 0.8110 as RBA cash rate expectations were repriced following
the RBA Board meeting and Australian CPI. In the short term, the
bias for NZD/AUD could be lower, given our view that the RBNZ
will pause at its late-October meeting and that the market is

under-pricing the risk of an RBA rate hike in November.
Next week’s QSBO, due on Tuesday, will be the key event
risk, with the inflation and growth gauges closely watched for
signs that might prompt the RBNZ to pause or hike. Markets
had previously priced in a high chance of a hike at the RBNZ’s
October meeting, but in recent days appear to be reassessing
that view.  Pricing for a 25bp hike in New Zealand could be
pared back towards a 50:50 call. The 0.8030–0.8150 range

is likely to contain the pair in the immediate period ahead.
Our medium-term view is for NZD/AUD to rise as the RBNZ
increases the OCR to 4% by the second half of 2027. We’ll
update our views on the NZD and other currencies in our next
Market Outlook, out Wednesday 14 October.
Fiscal update
The big uncertainty on the domestic horizon is, of course,
November’s general election, and at this stage the polling
looks tight. Neither the established left nor right bloc has a

clear majority.
Ahead of the election, the Treasury has released its Pre-election
Economic and Fiscal Update (PREFU), updating the Budget
2026 (BEFU) fiscal forecasts to reflect the latest economic and
fiscal information and a revised economic outlook. This allows
the public to assess the state of the Government’s finances. It
also provides other parties with a baseline against which to
calibrate their spending and taxation policies.
The PREFU revealed a stronger starting point for the

Government’s finances than had been expected in the Budget.
• A deficit of $8.6bn is estimated for 2025/26, down from the
Budget estimate of $11.9bn.
• Similarly, the forecast deficit for 2026/27 has been revised
down significantly to $6.8bn from $11.4bn.
• Thereafter, the OBEGALx deficit is expected to continue
declining gradually, moving to a surplus of $4bn in 2028/29.
This broadly follows the Budget profile, but is stronger
across the forecast period.

• Net core Crown debt is forecast to peak at 43.9% of GDP in
2027/28, below the 46.1% peak forecast in the BEFU. It’s then
forecast to decline to 39.5% of GDP by the end of the forecast
period in 2030/31, in line with the Government’s goal of
reducing debt below 40% of GDP over the medium term.
Given the improved outlook, NZDM announced a $4bn
reduction in the 2026/27 bond programme to $30bn and a
$15bn reduction in the forecast programme across the whole

period through to 2029/30.
The medium-term forecasts in the PREFU should be treated
with caution. The economic forecasts underpinning the
PREFU were finalised on 21 August, when both oil prices
and, especially, bond yields were materially lower than they
are today. Had the forecasts been finalised more recently, the
fiscal projections presented today would have been weaker,
at least in the near term. Indeed, the Treasury is upfront in
acknowledging that, while the risks are two-sided, the balance

is skewed to the downside.
Aside from the obvious uncertainty surrounding the economic
outlook because of unpredictable developments in the Middle
East, the approaching General Election, and the possibility of
a change in the composition of the Government, means the
assumptions underpinning the PREFU forecasts could quickly
become invalidated.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment (index)
Oct 6, Last: 84.4
Consumer sentiment declined 5.2% to 84.4 in September,
taking the index back towards the deeply pessimistic levels seen
earlier in the year. Rising fuel prices and fears of an imminent

interest rate rise drove the move with a continued weakening in
housing markets likely adding to unease in some quarters.
Developments over the last month would have added to these
concerns with: the RBA raising the cash rate by 25bps to 4.6%
during the survey week; average pump prices pushing above
$2.36/litre nationally and no end in sight to global supply
disruptions;  and more weak updates on the housing market.
NZ: Q2 NZIER Survey of Business Opinion (index)

Oct 6, General business confidence last: 12.3
Business conditions picked up modestly in the June quarter as
concerns about the war in the Middle East eased. However,
cost and pricing gauges remained firm. The September quarter
survey will be closely watched ahead of the RBNZ’s 28
October meeting. Recent weeks have seen signs that activity
is firming. However, fuel prices have also risen sharply, and we
(along with the RBNZ) will be watching for signs of a broader

lift in inflation pressures. The timing of survey responses
within the sample period will also warrant scrutiny, given that
oil prices rose late in the quarter.

Document 293: Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Following the oil shock, El Niño threatens to hurt macroeconomic resilience in Sub-Saharan Africa further (2026-10-21).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-21
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

21 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FOLLOWING THE OIL SHOCK, EL NIÑO THREATENS TO HURT MACROECONOMIC RESILIENCE IN SUB-SAHARAN
AFRICA FURTHER
Lucas Plé
Seven months after the start of the war in the Middle East, which has led to a sharp rise in oil prices, sub-Saharan Africa is holding up well
overall. The deterioration in external accounts and the rise in inflation are more contained than in 2022, during the last oil shock. However,
this resilience is fragile and is eroding as the conflict drags on. Furthermore, an exceptional El Niño event could significantly worsen the

macroeconomic environment in 2027 1.
Economic growth remains robust
For countries with available data, GDP growth held up well in the first half of 2026 2. In mid-October, the IMF revised its regional forecast for
the full year upwards to 4.5% (+0.2 percentage points compared with April), in line with the performance for 2025.
1 El Niño 2026–2027: multiple pressures on prices and supplies
2 1.4% year-on-year (y/y) in South Africa, 4.2% in Nigeria, 5.1% in Kenya, 7% in Angola, 7.3% in Ghana.

Sub-Saharan Africa is highly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 21 October 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A mitigated shock on terms of trade
High prices for export commodities (gold, copper, platinum) have helped to cushion the impact on terms of trade and the external
accounts of hydrocarbon-importing countries. For the 10 countries in our sample (see heatmap), the depreciation of their currencies
against the US dollar since last February has been moderate overall (2.3% on average).

Inflation has started to rise again, but it is still being contained so far
In the ten countries in our sample, although inflation is rising again, it is lower year-on-year for the first eight months of 2026 than it was
for the same period in 2022. This is due to:
1. Monetary policies that were still restrictive before the conflict started;
2. Fuel subsidies;
3.   Macroeconomic reforms implemented in recent years (a return to orthodox monetary policy in Nigeria, and efforts towards fiscal

consolidation in many countries);
4. Moderate currency depreciation since February.
As a result, few central banks have tightened their monetary policy since February (South Africa, Uganda and Tanzania).
However, this resilience is still fragile…
On the one hand, governments that have chosen to cap fuel price rises are seeing their public finances deteriorate. In Senegal, energy
subsidies will go above 3% of GDP in 2026, which is unsustainable. On the other hand, the region’s external accounts are exposed to

volatile global commodity prices and tighter international financing conditions.
… and the macroeconomic environment could quickly worsen in 2027, due to an exceptional El Niño event
The region is particularly vulnerable to climate shocks via the agricultural commodities channel. Given agriculture’s high share in its GDP ,
sub-Saharan Africa is more exposed to downside risks to growth than Latin America, Asia or Central Europe (1 st column of the heatmap).

It is also more exposed than other regions of the world to the inflationary effect of rising food prices, given their higher share in the
consumer basket (2 nd column). Finally, even though its dependence on food imports is lower than in Asia and Europe (3 rd column), the
region is highly vulnerable to rising international food commodity prices due to its fragile external accounts. For several countries in our
sample, foreign exchange reserves cover less than four months’ worth of imports of goods and services (Uganda, Tanzania, Ghana and

Zambia), leaving little room for manoeuvre for absorbing a further external shock.
In addition to shocks via the agricultural commodities channel, other localised risks must be considered. In Southern Africa, El Niño is
expected to intensify droughts and threaten electricity generation as a result, particularly in Zambia (where hydropower accounts
for nearly 80% of domestic electricity generation). In East Africa, the increased flooding that El Niño is expected to cause will threaten

infrastructure – particularly logistics infrastructure – which is already less efficient than the global average 3. These two sources of
vulnerability could also increase pressure on growth, prices and the external accounts.
In conclusion, if oil prices remain high and El Niño is exceptionally severe, the region will struggle to absorb this double shock in 2027.
Despite their status as net exporters of hydrocarbons, Nigeria and Angola are also exposed to a deterioration in the macroeconomic

environment in 2027. Only South Africa appears to be better positioned to absorb the shock.
lucas.ple@bnpparibas.com
3 See the World Bank’s Logistics Performance Index.

SEPTEMBER CPI PREVIEW
Setting the pace for the run into year end
19 October 2026
Key points
• September CPI to rise 0.62%mth 
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr 
from 4.0%yr. The seasonally adjusted 
CPI lifts 0.5%mth, a step down from 
the 0.7% increase in August.  
• September Trimmed Mean (TM) is 
set to lift 0.26%mth holding the 
annual pace steady at 3.6%yr. 
• As we noted in our review of the 
August CPI, our September quarter 
CPI estimate is unchanged at 1.3%qtr 
while the TM remained 0.9%qtr.  
• Auto fuel surged ahead of our 
expectations so along with signs of 
increase pass-through we lifted our 
December quarter CPI to 1.3%qtr/3.9%yr 
and the TM estimate to 0.9%qtr/3.5%yr. 
• This is a 0.8ppt increase to the CPI 
estimate, and a 0.2ppt increase to the 
TM estimate, highlighting inflation 
momentum meaningfully above 
the RBA’s current expectations.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
August inflation came in soft...
Inflation moderated in August following July’s strong 
outcome. The CPI rose 0.4% in the month, in line with our 
expectation but slightly below market consensus of 0.5%, 
taking the annual rate to 4.0%. On a seasonally adjusted 
basis, the CPI increased 0.7%mth, underscoring the impact of 
seasonal discounting in August and the start of the financial 
year repricing in July.
More closely watched are the measures of underlying inflation 
including the Trimmed Mean (TM) and market services. 
The TM rose 0.2% in August, in line with our expectations 
but again softer than the market, leaving the annual pace 
unchanged at 3.6% for a third consecutive month. Meanwhile, 
market services excluding volatiles & holiday travel increased 
0.2%mth/4.1%yr. Notable strength (three-month annualised, 
seasonally adjusted) was seen in sports participation (10.9%), 
clothing repair (7.0%), restaurant meals (6.8%) and other 
household services (5.2%). For full analysis of the August CPI, 
see “August CPI – Came in on our expectations ”.
The expenditure-weighted share of the CPI basket 
experiencing positive price shocks eased to 11% in August 
from 15% in July, while the share experiencing negative price 
shocks declined from 17% to just below 13%. The majority of 
the positive shocks were concentrated in consumer durables 
On a three-month moving average basis, the net momentum 
share (positive minus negative) rose to –4.5% from –7.7% 
in July and a trough of –15.8% in May, suggesting the 
disinflationary impulse may be nearing exhaustion. 
... with September due for a moderate pick-up
Looking ahead to September, early indicators painted a 
mixed picture. The NAB business survey showed retail price 
growth slowing to 0.9%qtr from 1.1%qtr in August, with 
labour cost growth easing to 1.7%qtr from 1.8%qtr, while 
purchase cost growth edged up to 2.4%qtr from 2.3%qtr. 
Nevertheless, all measures remain well above their long-run 
averages while the gap between purchase costs and final 
prices continues to widen, indicating businesses are facing 
margin compression (see here for further detail). Meanwhile, 
consumers’ year-ahead inflation expectations held steady 
at 4.9% in September (easing slightly at 2dp) but surged to 
5.3% in October likely reflecting higher fuel prices, with retail 
petrol and diesel averaging $2.20/l (+8.2%mth) and $2.68/l 
(+7.7%mth). We do believe the auto fuel prices are likely to see 
a near term peak in October.  Meanwhile, global shipping costs 
have also surged recently amid renewed Middle East tensions.
Sep quarter unrevised but Dec upgraded significantly
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The September CPI is expected to rise 0.6% (0.62% to 2dp)
with the annual pace lifting to 4.1%yr. Our seasonally adjusted
estimate increases 0.5% (0.48% to 2dp). For the monthly
TM, we expect a 0.3% (0.26% to 2dp) lift, holding the annual

pace steady at 3.6%. On a 3m/3m basis, the TM is tracking
a 1.0% gain in September (noting that while this measure is
conceptually different to the quarterly TM it still serves as a
useful guide). For market services excluding volatiles & holiday
travel we have pencilled in another 0.2% rise, slowing the
annual pace slightly to 4.0% (a nine-month low).
The largest contributors are recreation & culture (+0.27ppt),
driven wholly by holiday travel & accommodation. Transport

(+0.23ppt) makes a solid contribution, with gains in fuel
(+0.26ppt) partly offset by motor vehicles (–0.02ppt). Housing
also contributes (+0.13ppt), with rents and new dwellings
each adding 0.02ppt while property rates add 0.08ppt.
The key detractors in the month are food & non-alcoholic
beverages (–0.08ppt) and garments (–0.04ppt).
Key details for the month of September
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to fall
0.5%mth, easing the annual pace to 2.8%yr. The weakness is

concentrated in a 3.7%mth decline in fruit & vegetables, while
non-alcoholic beverages are also expected to fall 1.5%mth.
Meals out & takeaway is forecast to rise 0.1%mth, easing the
annual rate to 3.9%.
Housing is expected to rise 0.6% in September, slowing the
annual pace to 5.5%yr. Component-wise:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.3%mth and 3.7%yr. CPI rent
inflation is nearing its peak, consistent with the moderation
in asking rent growth. We expect rent inflation to ease to

3.6%yr by end-2026 and 2.5%yr by end-2027.
• New dwelling prices, which capture only the building
component of construction costs, are expected to rise
0.2%mth and 4.9%yr. Ongoing weakness in established
housing markets would be increasing builders’ difficulty in
passing through higher costs to new builds. As a result, we
expect new dwelling inflation to moderate to 4.5%yr by
end-2026 and 2.1%yr by end-2027.
Transport inflation is expected to rise 2.0%mth in September,

lifting the annual pace to 7.7%yr reflecting an 8.3%mth
increase in fuel prices following higher oil prices through the
month. Motor vehicle prices and spare parts are expected to
fall 0.7%mth and 1.3%mth, respectively. In NSW, a one-year
vehicle registration discount between $80-100 came into
effect from 1 September, which is likely to weigh on the other
services in respect of motor vehicles component.
Recreation & culture is expected to rise 2.1%mth driven by a

4.4%mth increase in holiday travel, partly offset by a 0.4%
decline in audio, visual & computing equipment & services and
a 1.3% fall in sporting services.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Risks to September baseline
We view the risks around the September CPI outcome as broadly 
balanced. Waning spending momentum outside of fuel may make 
it difficult for businesses to pass through higher costs in the near 
term. However, some of these pricing pressures may be deferred 
rather than forgone. The October ban on card surcharging may 
prompt a greater-than-usual degree of price resetting, allowing 
businesses to recover some of the margin pressures that have built 
up in recent months.
Risks to housing are mixed. Trade materials suppliers continued 
to announce above-average price increases through September 
(around 8%). While soft conditions in the established housing 
market may limit the extent to which these costs are passed 
through to new dwelling prices, they nevertheless present an 
upside risk to housing-related inflation, particularly maintenance 
& repair and insurance costs. Growth in advertised rents also 
continues to ease but these new leases take some time to flow 
through to average rents paid measured in the CPI. 
A kick up in October ...
We have a solid preliminary estimate for the October CPI of 
0.6%mth lifting the annual pace to 4.8%yr. It is true that the key 
driver is an 8.9% increase in auto fuel but we estimate the TM to 
lift 0.4%mth/3.7%yr highlighting broader inflationary pressures. 
We expect that the removal of credit charge surcharges and 
resulting increase in retail prices, at most, will contribute 0.1ppt 
but only just so see it as more of a rounding error. Nevertheless, 
these changes come at a time of a significant upstream price 
pressure, particularly for SMEs, so there is some risk of broader 
price adjustments. As such, we expect marginally larger than 
usual price adjustments in October particularly in hospitality 
and household services. Being the first month of the quarter the 
October lift sets a higher base for the quarterly average estimates 
resulting in an upgrade to our December quarter estimate.
We have included a table with a monthly profile for the CPI, 
seasonally adjusted CPI and the TM out to June 2027. At 
June 2027 we have an annual pace of 3.7%yr for the CPI and 
seasonally adjusted CPI and 3.3%yr for the TM. There is also a 
table breaking down the monthly components of the CPI through 
the December quarter. 
... highlights stronger inflationary pressure ...
Our September quarter CPI estimate is unrevised at 1.3%qtr 
with the annual pace steady at 3.9%yr. At the margin there 
was a 0.04ppt increase due to a lift in auto fuel but this is 
1.29%qtr at two decimal places so still rounds to 1.3%. 
The seasonally adjusted CPI gains 1.1% in the September 
quarter highlighting that part of the increase is due to usual 
seasonality. Our TM estimate for Q3 has a small downward 
revision of 0.03ppt to 0.88% at two decimal place so our 
published estimate is also unrevised at 0.9%qtr. 
... resulting in an upward revision to Q4 2026
Westpac’s December quarter CPI estimate has been boosted 
from a 0.5%qtr to 1.3%qtr. Driving most of this change from 
our previous estimate is the 15.3% increase in autofuel, it

was 1.9%, contributing 0.55ppt to the 0.8ppt increase in the

A disinflationary impulse kicks in for 2027
We now expect auto fuel prices to peak in October (240 cpl 
for petrol, 284 cpl for diesel) and then to trend lower through 
2027 (184 cpl for petrol and 194 cpl for diesel by December 
2027) leading to a 13.6% falling in auto fuel in the first half 
of 2027 subtracting 0.46ppt off the CPI over the same period 
of time. For a detailed description on how we constructed the 
auto fuels profile please see: “ Persistent energy pressures 
have further to run ” 
The first half of 2027 will also see an almost 5% decline in 
holiday travel & accommodation which will subtract a further 
0.29ppt off the CPI. However, outside of that any broader 
moderation in inflation is expected to be more muted hence 
we have the TM printing 0.7%qtr for each quarter in 2027.  
Critical to this moderation in core inflation is our expect step 
down in dwelling price inflation on the back of falling estab -
lished home prices. From a 1.1%qtr increase in Q3 2026 we 
see dwelling price increasing by 0.7% in the Q4 2026, and 
then 0.6% in both Q1 and Q2 2027 before dropping to 0.5%qtr 
in Q3 and Q4 2027. This will see dwelling price inflation in the 
CPI drop from 5.4%yr at June 2026 to 3.0%yr by June 2027 
then down to 2.2%yr by December 2027. 
At least for the near term a key uncertainty remains the extent 
to which businesses are able to pass through ongoing labour 
and non-labour cost pressures and how this unwinds through 
2027 as energy prices ease and the domestic economy slows 
under the weight of higher interest rates and falling estab -
lished home prices. There is a risk that firms face greater 
resistance to price increases if discretionary household spend -
ing softens by more than expected. That said, these headwinds 
may be at least partially offset by the stronger starting point 
for household incomes, which has proven more resilient than 
previously anticipated, and by higher energy prices due to the 
ongoing conflict in the Middle East.
That said, there is also the risk that the disinflationary pulse by 
the second half of 2027 could be greater than what we antici-

level if not lower, then headline inflation could dip below 2%yr 
and the TM would be closer to 2.5%yr (the mid point if the 
RBA’s inflation target), by end 2027.

美股個股報告

營業比重(3Q26)

個股 與指數走勢 (%)

資料來源：Bloomberg 
賴彥維 
Benson.lai@ctbcsis.com

航太  
SpaceX  (SPCX ) 
首次 評等  
買進 (Buy) 
財報、財測 大幅優於預期， SI(AI) 成為最大業務  
⚫ 財報、財測皆顯著優於市場共識 
⚫ SI 部門成為第一大業務，且轉虧為盈  
⚫ Starlink 用 戶數穩定成長  
財報、財測顯著優於市場共識，並實現虧轉盈：SpaceX 於 10/20 舉行財
報會議， FY3Q26 營收 146 億美元 (QoQ+87% ，YoY+ 161 %)，優於市
場共識 129 億美元， GAAP 毛利率 62.4% ，EPS 0.35 美元，顯著優於
市場共識 0.15 美元，且由虧轉盈。公司本次維持 2026 年底年化經常
性收入 (ARR) 目標 1,000 億美元不變，並給予 1Q27 營收目標為
330~370 億美元，大幅優於市場共識 244 億美元，公司優異財報及財
測表現使盤後股價上漲 8%。 
SI(AI) 部門成為第一大業務，且轉虧為盈： SI 部門 FY3Q26 營收 76
億美元，首次成為公司最大業務，主要受惠雲端服務合約及 Grok Bot
帶動。其中 SI 解決方案與基礎建設業務營收 70 億美元，廣告營收
5.4 億美元 (YoY+19%) 。部門調整後 EBITDA 為 50 億美元，營業利
潤 4.31 億美元，由 FY2Q26 營業虧損 13 億美元轉虧為盈。公司目前
算力規模已達 2.2 GW ，預期年底將達 2.5~3GW ，目前每月上線約 20
萬台 GB300 ，2027 年目標則為 13 GW ，將以 Vera Rubin 為主，預期
FY2Q27 末將上線 44 萬台 VR 伺服器。  
Starlink 用 戶數每年高速成長： Connectivity 部門 FY3Q26 營收 52
億美元 (YoY+69% ，QoQ+22%) ，毛利率 55% ，FY3Q26 消費用戶數
達 1,360 萬，淨增加 160 萬用戶。 ARPU(Average Revenue Per User)
方面， FY3Q26 ARPU 69 美元 /月(QoQ+5%) ，低於 FY3Q25 的 83 美
元/月，主因為公司服務範圍由原先歐美轉向南美、非洲、東南亞等
地區，因此在定價策略上進行調整。 Starlink Mobile 手機直連業務月
活躍 用戶突破 2,200 萬(QoQ+37%) ，已獲 FCC 批准發射 15,000 顆
Mobile V2 衛星並收購低頻段頻譜，預計 2028 年初開通服務。  
評價： 我們認為 SpaceX 於太空產業位處龍頭地位，且目前並未有競
爭對手，加上 Grok 帶來強勁成長動能 ，因此 首次 評等 給予「買進」，
評價面考量公司位於高速成長初期，加上公司業務多為訂閱模式，
以 PS 方式給予評價 ，目標價 220 美元(2027 每股營收 10.8 美元 × 20
倍 PSR)。風險因子 :低軌衛星用戶數不如預期、 AI 資料中心建置需
求轉弱 。

美股個股報告

2 
SpaceX 為全球 太空產業龍頭 ：SpaceX 業務主要分為 Connectivity 、AI
及 Space 三大部門。 Connectivity 以 Starlink 衛星連網服務為核心，涵
蓋消費者、企業及政府客戶業務，包括 Starshield 相關服務； SI(Super 
Intelligence) 部門以 SpaceXSI( 前 xAI) 為核心，涵蓋 AI 運算服務、 Grok
及 X(前 Twitter) 平台 ；Space 部門則涵蓋 火箭 發射服務與太空計畫開發
業務。公司 FY3Q26 業務比重分別為 Connectivity 36%、SI 52%、Space 
12% 。 Space X 在衛星通訊與火箭發射領域均具領先地位 ， 2025 年
SpaceX 共發射 165 次火箭， 全 球 火 箭 發 射共 325 次， 其 市 占 率 約
50.8% ，而在衛星通訊領域， Starlink 則為目前唯一可提供寬頻衛星通
訊服務給大規模用戶的供應商。  
圖表一：衛星產業市場規模

資料來源： SIA ，中信投顧整理  
Falcon 9 開啟火箭技術革命 ： 傳統 火箭多為一次性 設計 ，主要結構於
發 射後 拋棄， 使每 次發射 任務 均須重 新製 造與整 備， 導致 火 箭發 射成
本高昂。而 SpaceX 的獵鷹 9 號(Falcon 9 )於 2015 年首次完成一級火箭
回收，並於 2017 年實現回收助推器再次飛行，突破火箭一次性使用限
制，降低火箭發射成本， 使衛星寬頻通訊商業化可行性提高。 Falcon 9
每次發射衛星 報價 約在 3,200 美元 /公斤 ，SpaceX 自用成本更低 ，而最
新 的傳 統火 箭(Ariane 6) 發射 報價 則 約在 6,000 美元 /公斤 ， 相比 之 下
SpaceX 在火箭發射成本上有顯著優勢。 而 下 一 代 火 箭星艦 (Starship)
推測每公斤成本約在 600 美元 /公斤， 且目標 為全部回收並 將發射成本
進一步降低至 200 美元 /公斤 ，有望進一步帶動衛星發射數量 。

入軌道，同時成功 佈署 26 顆 Starlink V3 衛星 。惟任務過程仍出現若
干 異常 ，上層 引擎 異常提 前關 閉 ，使 公司 一度放 棄入 軌計畫 ，成 功入

推時亦有一台引擎故障，顯示 Starship 距離穩定回收使用仍 需時間改
進。雖然 Starship 距離最終目標仍有一段距離 ， 但本次成功佈署 V3 衛
星對低軌衛星產業有正面意義 ，公司預期 2027 年中有望使 V3 在軌衛
星 數量 達數百 顆， 能使用 戶感 受到明 顯的 速度提 升與 降低延 遲。 公司
預期第 15 次試射時間落在下個月，將嘗試透過發射塔回收推進器與太
空船，若成功回收並再飛有望使低軌衛星產業迎來新一波成長期。

Space 部門 取得多項發射合約 ：Space 部門主要負責火箭發射服務與太

Islamic finance is growing at a rapid pace, driven 
primarily by increasing demand for investors who 
seek Shariah -compliant investments. Sukuk, often 
referred to as Islamic bonds, surpassed USD 1trn in 
2025, making them one of the fastest -growing 
segments o f Islamic finance. The asset class’s 
unique value proposition is driving its popularity, 
especially in an increasingly volatile global  
economic landscape. 
In this publication, we will explore the evolving role of Sukuk in wealth management, 
examining market fundamentals, risk frameworks and emerging opportunities within 
this market.

### Page 3

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  3

Sukuk, commonly referred to as Islamic bonds, are Shariah-compliant investment 
certificates representing ownership interests in underlying assets, usufructs (the 
right to use an asset and receive its benefits, without owning it) or ventures. In 
return, Sukuk holders receive periodic distributions, typically in the form of rental 
payments (Ijarah), profit-sharing (Mudarabah) or asset-generated cash flows, and 
upon maturity the principal amount is repaid. Sukuk differ from conventional 
bonds in several ways: 
Fig. 1  
Sukuk vs. conventional bonds

Aspect  Conventional bonds Sukuk

Core principle  Lending (time-value of money) Asset ownership 
Underlying asset  Any asset, business or project Must be Sharia-compliant 
Returns  Guaranteed (fixed or floating interest) Not legally guaranteed (depends on 
underlying asset’s performance) 
Risk   Issuer bears all performance risk Shared risk between parties 
Transparency  Moderate (depends on disclosure) High (asset identification required)

Source: Standard Chartered

The role of Sukuk in the investing landscape is evolving amid 
global uncertainty and rising geopolitical tensions. Investors 
are increasingly seeking more stable and asset ‑backed 
alternatives. An investor can consider investing in Sukuk for 
the following reasons: 
Resilient 
Sukuk provide asset -backed collateral that conventional 
bonds often lack, offering a cushion during volatile market 
conditions. Historically, Islamic investments, including Sukuk’, 
have been countercyclical, i.e. they tend to expand or remain 
stable when c onventional markets contract. Across major 
market stress from the 2018 US-China Trade War through the 
2026 Middle East Conflict, Sukuk bonds consistently 
outperformed Global Bonds — delivering smaller drawdowns 
in risk-off episodes such as the 2018 US-China trade war and 
2022 higher for longer inflation, and greater resilience in 
geopolitical oil shocks, underscoring the asset class's 
structural defensive characteristics.

1 The evolving role of Sukuk

Strong fundamentals
The Sukuk market experienced an unprecedented 25% growth in issuance in 2025, with issuance nearly USD 300bn. According
to some  reports,  the  Sukuk  market  could  reach  USD  3trn  by  2030  driven  by  increased  demand  for  Shariah-compliant
investments, sovereign funds’ diversification needs, development of regulatory frameworks for Sukuk and growth in Middle East

capital markets.  Nearly  80%  of  Fitch ‑rated Sukuk  carry  investment ‑grade ratings  and  have  little  or  no  history  of  default,
underscoring the sector’s strong fundamentals. These factors should help to instil confidence among investors, who can look at
Sukuk as central building blocks of their portfolio.

Convergence with sustainable investing trends
Sukuk and sustainable bonds share more common ground than their different origins suggest, making sustainable Sukuk a
natural bridge between Islamic finance and sustainable investing. This also helps explain Sukuk’s growing appeal beyond
traditional Muslim investors, as evidenced by Sustainable Sukuk issuance rising from USD 464mn in 2017 to USD 9bn in 2024,

representing a CAGR of 52.7%. The overlap starts with what Sukuk exclude: Shariah principles prohibit interest, excessive
uncertainty and speculation, and screen out sectors financing harmful industries such as tobacco, gambling, weapons, adult
entertainment and alcohol – a negative screen that closely aligns with the exclusionary (negative screening) method, commonly
used in sustainable investing. Tradi tional Sukuk bonds require proceeds to be linked to identifiable real assets or projects,

creating a ring-fenced structure like use-of-proceeds sustainable bonds. Combined with Shariah’s broader objectives of social
welfare, resource stewardship and equita ble risk-sharing, this creates strong alignment with the United Nations Sustainable
Development Goals.
Green Sukuk is an established example, where proceeds are earmarked for environmental projects such as renewable energy,
clean transport, energy-efficient buildings or sustainable water and wastewater management. Issuers must meet both Shariah

approval from a recognised scholarly board and alignment with International Capital Market Association (ICMA) Green Bond
Principles.
Traditional Sukuk already comply with Shariah laws such as avoiding Riba (interest -based returns arising from lending
transactions), Gharar (investments with ambiguity) and Maysir (speculation). Sustainable Sukuk build on this foundation by
adding environmental and social objectives. The table below highlights how sustainable Sukuk differ from traditional Sukuk.

Fig. 4
Traditional Sukuk vs. Sustainable bonds vs Sustainable Sukuk
Aspect Traditional Sukuk Sustainable Bonds Sustainable Sukuk
Underlying structure Asset-linked
Use of proceeds For any Shariah-compliant
purpose

Digital integration
The integration  of  blockchain  technology  and  digital  platforms  is  revolutionising  Sukuk  issuance  and  trading,  dramatically
reducing transaction costs and expanding accessibility to  a broader  investor base.  For instance, SAKKEX (an  independent
blockchain-based Sukuk marketplace) streamlines drafting, issuance, and near ‑instant settlement. Its Sukuk Wallet App also
allows investors to trade on the go. Several such digital platforms now allow tokenised Sukuk that enable fractional ownership,

automated Shariah  compliance  verification  and  real-time  settlement,  thereby  addressing  challenges  that  have  persisted  in
Islamic finance markets. Digital tools also simplify cross border Sukuk transactions by enabling end to end digital origination and
trading, with smart contracts automating profit sharing to cut operational costs and enhance transparency.

### Page 6

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026 6
Sukuk structures define the nature of ownership, risk -sharing and return
generation. Several types of Sukuk structures exist and are defined by the
underlying Shariah contract. We discuss some prominent ones below and see
how they align with various phases of an investor’s wealth journey:
Sukuk al-Ijarah is a Shariah-compliant lease-based bond where the investor gains partial ownership in a physical

asset such as a building, airport or equipment. The asset is then leased back to the issuer who pays a nominal
(pre-agreed) rent to the investor. An investor seeking stable, predictable income streams with lower volatility can
consider investing in these Sukuk. The original principal amount is returned at the end of the lease period.
Ijarah Sukuk are ideal for the wealth preservation phase of an investor’s wealth journey. For instance, investors
nearing retirement typically need to restructure their portfolios by shifting from growth to preservation while

generating steady income to cover living expenses. Ijarah Sukuk deliver on both fronts: protecting capital through
asset-backed structures while providing predictable rental income that supports household needs without
depleting principal.
Sukuk al-Murabahah are cost-plus financing structures where the issuer buys an asset and immediately resells
it to the investors at a marked-up price, with payment deferred over a pre-determined time. Consider this: Let us

assume, a company needs USD 100m. A special purpose vehicle (SPV), established by the company, mobilises
funds from investors and buys commodities worth USD 100m and immediately resells them to the company for
USD 112m payable over time. The USD 12m difference is the agreed profit margin. Murabahah Sukuk ar e best
suited for investors seeking income over a short to medium term, typically, 1-5 years. Investments in this type of
Sukuk return the principal gradually throughout the investment period rather than only at maturity.

Investments in Murabahah Sukuk are best suited for wealth protection and income generation. Unlike Ijarah which
return principal as a lump sum at maturity, Murabahah amortise both profit and principal in each instalment. This
makes them ideal for retirees who need to systematically draw down capital to fund living expenses without
depleting their portfolio too quickly.
Sukuk al-Mudarabah is a profit-sharing partnership arrangement where investors provide 100% of the capital

and operate as silent partners. The issuer manages the business or project as the active partner. Under a
Mudarabah arrangement, financial losses arising from normal bu siness risks are borne by the investors in
proportion to their capital contribution, while the Mudarib loses its effort and entitlement to profit. However, where
losses result from negligence, misconduct, breach of agreed terms, or misconduct by the Mudari b, the Mudarib
may be held liable. Think of it as venture capital meets bonds; you fund the business, professionals run it and

you share the profits or bear the losses resulting from the business activities. Mudarabah Sukuk are often
regarded as one of the  structures most closely aligned with the Shariah principle of profit and loss sharing.
However, they bear higher risk and lower predictability compared to other structures.
Investors with a growth mindset and open to high -risk tolerance can consider investing in these Sukuk . They
best align with accumulation and growth phase of the wealth journey, typically, when investors have long time

horizons to absorb volatility and can afford capital risk for higher returns. However, to balance risk, it is important
for investors to diversify across various types of Sukuk. Even within Mudarabah, they can consider investing
in multiple projects, sectors and geographies.
1 Sukuk structures and
wealth mandates

### Page 7

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  7

A joint venture partnership structure where both investors and the issuer contribute capital is Sukuk al -
Musharakah. In this structure, both parties (issuer and investors) jointly provide capital, participate in managing 
the venture, and share profits or losses based on agreed upon ratios. This structure appeals to investors focused 
on growth, interested in active participation, and prepared to take on greater risk in pursuit of higher returns. 
Musharakah Sukuk are best suited for the growth and accumulation phase where investors are building wealth 
aggressively, comfortable with high risk and able to lock up capital for the long term (typically 5+ years).

Sukuk al-Wakalah is an agency arrangement where investors appoint a representative from the issuing company 
as their Wakil (agent) to invest their funds in specific Shariah-compliant assets or projects in exchange for a fee. 
This arrangement is similar to a mutual fund or ETF, where the agent manages a diversified pool of Shariah 
compliant assets on behalf of the investors. Investors benefit from professional fund management, diversification, 
transparency and flexibility. Depending on issuer preferences, Wakalah Sukuk can b e structured with lower 
investment thresholds, making them accessible to a wide range of investors. 
Wakalah Sukuk suitability depends heavily on the underlying asset allocation and risk profile. Conservative 
Wakalah structures investing in stable, income -generating assets suit wealth preservation. Moderate -risk 
Wakalah can complement accumulation portfolios as a diversification tool, though not as a primary growth driver 
as their structure and risk mandate limit upside potential.

Sukuk al-Istisna is a project financing structure based on a manufacturing or construction contract. Investors 
provide upfront capital to finance the construction of specific assets. Upon completion, the asset is sold to an end 
buyer (often the original issuer or a third party) who makes deferred payments including the cost plus a mark-up, 
which are then distributed to investors. Istisna suit seasoned investors with construction finance expertise and 
those who can absorb development risk and commit capital for the long term. Owing to its complexity and higher 
risk profile, this type of Sukuk typically requires large initial investments, making it less accessible to retail 
investors.  
In the wealth cycle, Istisna are best aligned with the accumulation and growth phase, when long time horizons 
can absorb construction delays and development risks. The long-term commitment and potential for cost overruns 
or project failure make it unsuitable for later wealth stages such as retirement.

Sukuk al -Salam is an Islamic bond backed by a forward purchase contract where investors pay upfront for 
commodities to be delivered later. At its core, they are similar to forward contracts. However, unlike a forward 
contract, Salam contracts are fully prepaid, standardi sed and designed with clearly defined delivery terms to 
reduce uncertainty (gharar). Under this arrangement, an investor profits from the difference between the lower 
Salam purchase price (paid today) and the higher resale price (received at delivery).  
Salam Sukuk carry high delivery and price risk and aligns well with investors in the growth and accumulation 
phase. Salam requires strong commodity‑market expertise, deep understanding of production and delivery risks, 
and the ability to assess the issuer’s operational capacity to deliver the specified goods on time.

Owing to their ethical framework and asset -backed nature, Sukuk are often
considered relatively less risky than conventional bonds. However, they are not
immune to risks. Unique challenges such as asset quality deterioration and
structure-specific exposures require rigorous risk management.

Due diligence in Sukuk extends beyond traditional credit
analysis and an investor should also consider the following:
• Shariah compliance verification can be done by evaluating

the Sukuk structure and confirming that its mandates and
underlying assets fully align with Islamic principles.
• Evaluating asset quality by verifying tangibility, cash -
generating capability and legal ownership can help
investor reduce risk.
• Assessing creditworthiness and market depth will help in
identifying financially strong Sukuk issuers and ensuring
adequate liquidity.
• Cross-border Sukuk investments remain viable,
contingent on careful scrutiny of legal documentation

across multiple jurisdictions to ensure enforceability and
investor protection.
Robust due diligence and proactive risk mitigation strategies
are essential to protect capital and stay aligned with Islamic
financial principles.
1 Risk management and due diligence

### Page 10

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  10

Staying up to date on the latest developments in 
the Sukuk market is important as this space is 
constantly evolving and rapidly expanding. 
Moreover, Islamic finance is not static; Shariah 
interpretations related to Sukuk structures are 
periodically redefined. Staying informed protects 
investors from investing in non -compliant 
options. We look at some of the factors that are 
guiding the future of this landscape:

1 Emerging trends

Green Sukuk issuance is projected to grow materially through 
2030, reflecting the natural convergence of Islamic finance and 
sustainability principles. Key sectors include renewable 
energy, green buildings, low -carbon transportation, water and 
waste management and sustainable agriculture.

Tech advancements  are revolutionising Sukuk origination, 
trading and accessibility. Digitisation offers numerous benefits 
to the Islamic finance landscape: streamlined issuance and 
trading processes, enhanced accessibility through 
tokenisation, immutable audit trails via bl ockchain technology 
and improved secondary market liquidity.

A major regulatory shift is likely to create standardised rules 
across Islamic finance markets globally. The pivotal 
development is AAOIFI's Standard 62, launching in 2026, 
which mandates transparent, Shariah-compliant asset-backing 
and genuine asset ownership. This can fundament ally 
strengthen the Islamic authenticity of Sukuk structures.

The Sukuk market is rapidly expanding beyond its traditional 
Islamic finance strongholds. Non-traditional territories  in 
Africa (e.g., Algeria, Benin and Nigeria), Europe (e.g., 
Luxemburg, UK) and Asia (e.g., Turkey, Pakistan) are 
emerging as Islamic capital market gateways. This expansion 
underscores the evolution of Sukuk from a niche Islamic 
product to a globally reco gnised asset class attracting diverse 
sovereigns and investors across continents.

### Page 11

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  11

Conclusion

Ultimately, Sukuk demonstrate that Shariah compliance and sound investment 
strategy are not competing priorities, but complementary ones. By ensuring that 
portfolios are grounded in real economic activity by offering stability through 
tangible asset -backing and transparent structures that channel capital towards 
productive use. Their foundation in ethical, responsible financing appeals to 
investors seeking both resilience and purpose. As global markets increasingly 
prioritise integrity and long-term sustainability, Sukuk stand out as a compelling tool 
for Shariah -compliant investors who want meaningful diversification without 
compromising on their religious and ethical principles.

月以來的首次升息 。預料2026年底再升息一碼 ，
其後進入觀察期。

線估值有壓 ：美債殖利率通常要等確認升息到頂
後才會回落 ，市場往往在升息前因政策不確定性
而較為震盪；然而 ，一旦後續經濟數據確認景氣
仍處於擴張軌道 ，即使估值面受到利率壓抑 ，企

業獲利成長仍有望成為推動股市續漲的主要動能 。

聯準會升息落地，股債市未見明顯衝擊
全球投資級債因利率上升而承壓，非投資級債穩健
盤勢回顧
股市情緒受到影響但未失控
3
• 升息預期推高公債及信用債殖利率，但升息落地後有趨穩現象：早在聯準會利率會議召開前夕，市場不斷出現
升息聲浪，推高10年期公債利率一度站上5%，壓抑9月以來信用債市表現，尤其以投資級債表現最為弱勢，也
拖累全年總報酬率轉負 ，但利差走勢其實相當平穩 ，８月以來美投資級債利差大致在 75~85基點區間震盪 。
FOMC會議後公債利率略自高檔回落，並沒有因為升息而持續創高，信用債市殖利率也趨於穩定。
• 股市情緒受到影響但未失控 ：9月以來股市情緒受到一定程度影響 ，不過並未出現恐慌情緒 ，僅利率敏感性類
股震盪較大，非核心消費之外的類股年至今仍多收正報酬。聯準會升息落地後，股市單日跌幅也可控。
資料來源：左， Bloomberg ，2026/9/17，右，Bloomberg，2026/9/17。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/17

### Page 4

©  CTBC
風險管理式升息，而非典型的升息循環重新啟動
2026年底前預期還有一次升息
貨幣政策
通膨廣度其實已有所收斂，預期此一趨勢將延續
4
• 風險管理式升息 ，而非典型的升息循環重新啟動 ：9月FOMC會議全票通過升息一碼至 3.75%-4.0%，這是自
2023年7月以來的首次升息。本次會議的重點可歸納如下：(1)利率預估點陣圖顯示至2026年底前還有一碼的升息
空間；(2)上調通膨與經濟成長預估值，同時下調失業率，是一個很鷹派的組合，不過調整幅度其實很小，2026
年的整體PCE通膨和核心PCE通膨僅上調0.1個百分點；(3)判斷本次會議並非典型的升息循環重新啟動，而是「不
承諾路徑的風險管理式升息」。
• 年底前再升息一碼後進入觀察期 ：搭配利率預估點陣圖，以及本行對2027年通膨走勢的判斷(8月的CPI數據已
經看到通膨廣度已經有收斂態勢，搭配本次升息，預期此一趨勢將延續，2Q27通膨有望降至2.5%以下)，本行
基準情境認為年底前再升息一碼後，2027上半年進入按兵不動的觀察期，2027年下半年有望轉向寬鬆立場。

資料來源：左， Fed ，2026/9/17，右，Robin Brooks，2026/9。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9

©  CTBC 
推演殖利率4Q26偏高表現、1H27適度回落
市場初步反應: 長率未進一步上揚
美債利率
美債殖利率通常要等確認升息到頂後才會回落
5
• 聯準會升息證明公信力，美債市場大致正向表現：9月FOMC會議前市場已先定價升息，而使得美國10年期公債
殖利率來到5%以上，而在FOMC會議後短率上揚、長率大致穩定，顯示市場主要重新定價近期政策變動，而非同
步大幅上修長期通膨或長期利率風險，是一項相對正面的訊號。
• 推演殖利率4Q26偏高表現、1H27適度回落：根據歷史經驗，美債殖利率通常要等確認升息到頂後才會回落，
4Q26在鷹派論調+期中選舉+高油價的影響下，研判美國10年期公債殖利率維持偏高水位 。2027年隨通膨放
緩，市場確認停止升息後，美債殖利率將適度回落。預估未來兩季美國10年期公債殖利率的區間將處於4.5%-
5.1%，美國30年期公債殖利率則在 5.1%-5.5%之間。尾端風險是「後續的升息不斷超過市場預期 」，不過在
油價不持續失控向上的情況下，尾端風險的機率並不高。

過去一個月企業獲利預估值變化
AI成長趨勢
受惠戰爭
• 景氣擴張週期尚未結束 ，升息對股市影響有限 ：9月FOMC決議升息一碼，但股市反應相對有限 。一方面，市
場已提前消化升息預期；更重要的是，過去一年全球景氣維持穩定擴張，且前景仍偏正向。回顧過往景氣擴張
階段的升息循環，市場往往在升息前因政策不確定性而較為震盪；然而 ，一旦後續經濟數據確認景氣仍處於擴
張軌道，即使估值面受到利率壓抑，企業獲利成長仍有望成為推動股市續漲的主要動能。
• 估值壓縮，選股回到獲利能見度：在股市策略方面，現階段獲利成長動能最強的仍是受惠於 AI長期趨勢的科技
供應鏈。部分科技股已於7至8月率先修正整理，不僅評價壓力有所緩解，也提供較具吸引力的布局機會。不過，
中東地緣衝突尚未落幕，加上國際油價維持高檔，使國防、能源等受惠地緣局勢的族群，成為兼具主題性與避
險特性的配置選擇。整體而言，在聯準會重啟升息的環境下，上半年資金雨露均霑的普漲行情可能逐漸降溫，

但獲利能見度高、基本面持續改善的族群，仍可望獲得資金青睞並展現相對優勢。

### Page 7

©  CTBC
基本面穩健有助降低信用債波動，美元短線路徑轉強
利率走升環境下以非投資級債表現較穩定
市場觀點
資料來源：(左) Fed，2026/9/17；(右) ICE BofA indices，2026/9/16 。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/17
• 基本面穩健態勢不變，有助穩定信用債利差：FOMC會議證實美國經濟韌性，在景氣成長及企業獲利正向循環
未結束前，穩健債信將支持信用債利差走勢持穩，雖然目前美國公債利率已大致反應升息預期，但政策不確定
性仍牽動短線較大波動，利率敏感度較低的非投資級債預期將有相對突出的表現 ，而高利率及高債信品質的族
群則可長期持有以息收為主，而外幣債與美國雙政策連動性較低，新當債與澳幣債走勢可望回歸自身貨幣政策。
• 貨幣政策路徑上修帶動美元走強：美元已從2026年初的降息交易轉向受到美伊戰爭、油價上漲及通膨預期升溫
支撐，但此波強勢更多來自地緣政治與高利率環境 ，而非美國基本面出現結構性轉強 。Fed本次升息後美元指
數當日上升約0.6%，美元指數再度回測100。從聯準會官員利率點陣圖判斷，短期利率路徑較先前預期偏高，

支撐美元表現；惟在通膨回穩後 ，貨幣政策仍有機會重新轉向較寬鬆立場 。留意4Q26共和黨失去絕對多數後
對財政樽節的預期，美元短線路徑轉強。美元指數目標價從97上修至99。

Document 475: パリは燃えている、揺れるフランス政治 ～大規模抗議が揺さぶる財政再建と大統領選挙～

Source PDF: all report pdf/パリは燃えている、揺れるフランス政治 ～大規模抗議が揺さぶる財政再建と大統領選挙～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

Europe Trends

2026 年10 月 19 日

1/6 
パリは燃えている、揺れるフランス政治 
～大規模抗議が揺さぶる財政再建と大統領選挙～ 
 経済調査部 首席エコノミスト  田中 理 
（℡：050-5474-7494）

（要旨） 
○ フランスで学生の抗議運動を発端とする大規模なデモが広がり、一部で暴動に発展している。
教育環境の悪化、物価高による生活の厳しさ、公共サービスの質の低下が、政府への不信と
結びつき、労働組合や年金生活者など幅広い層を巻き込む社会運動に拡大した。 
○ 政府は 2027 年予算案で 540 億ユーロの財政改善措置を計画するが、歳出抑制への反発が強
まれば、野党との妥協は一段と難しくなる。抗議の長期化による経済活動の下押しや、金利
上昇による利払い負担の増加が、財政再建を一段と難しくする恐れもある。予算成立の遅れ
や財政再建策の後退は、国債市場の警戒感を高めかねない。 
○ 来春の大統領選に向けても、購買力や公共サービスの改善に加え、暴動への対応や治安維持
が重要な政策争点となる可能性がある。各党は政府の財政運営への批判を強める一方、予算
不成立による混乱をどこまで容認するかという難しい判断を迫られる。 
○ 今回の大規模抗議運動は、財政再建の必要性が高まる一方で、その負担を国民に求めるため
の政治的・社会的な合意形成が難しくなっているという構造的な問題を浮き彫りにした。来
春の大統領選で誰が政権を担うことになっても、財政規律の回復と国民生活の改善をいかに
両立させるかという難題から逃れることはできない。

■学生の抗議から全国的な社会運動へ 
フランスで大規模な抗議運動が広がっている。10 月 17 日には全国約110 ヶ所でデモが行われ、
参加者は内務省の集計で約55 万人、主催者側の発表では約80 万人に達した。パリだけでも約11
万人が参加し、リヨンやマルセイユなど主要都市でも大規模な抗議活動が行われた。デモの多く
は平和的に実施されたが、覆面姿の集団など一部が暴徒化し、警察と激しく衝突した。商店の略
奪や建物への放火が発生し、地方都市では市庁舎など公共施設も被害を受けた。警察による拘束
者は全国で2000 人を超え、警察官らにも多数の負傷者が出た。マクロン大統領は18 日夜、国民
向けの演説を行い、生活費や教育環境の改善を求める抗議に理解を示す一方、暴力行為を厳しく
非難した。政府は被害を受けた自治体や商店への緊急支援を進めるとともに、必要に応じて一部

### Page 2

2/6
の集会を禁止するなど、治安対策を強化する。社会的な不満への対応と公共秩序の回復を同時に
迫られる事態となっている。
発端となったのは、９月中旬にパリ郊外の学校で始まった高校生らによる抗議活動だった。一
部の学校では、新学期が始まっても担当教員が決まらず、授業を開始できなかったり、病気や休
職で教員が不在になっても代替教員を確保できず、授業が休講になったりする問題が発生してい
る。休講が重なることで授業の進度が遅れ、学力の習得や進学に向けた準備に支障が生じる。校
舎の老朽化や教育設備の不足、学級規模の大きさなどにも不満が広がっている。
こうした教育環境の改善を求める抗議活動は、ソーシャルメディアを通じて広がり、他の地域
の高校生や大学生も加わった。フランスの教育への公的支出は国際的にみて必ずしも低いわけで
はない。それにもかかわらず、学校現場での教員不足や施設の老朽化が問題となるのは、教員の
地域的な偏在や教育資源の配分など、構造的な要因があると考えられる。
特に生活費や住居費が高いパリ周辺や、低所得世帯が集中する地域では、教員の確保が難し

い。教員の給与水準は、同程度の学歴を持つ他の就業者に比べて低いことも、教職の魅力を損ね
る要因となっている。また、学校施設の整備については、中央政府だけでなく地方自治体も重要
な役割を担っており、小学校は市町村、中学校は県、高校は地域圏が主に所管している。老朽化
した校舎の改修に加えて、猛暑対策や省エネルギー改修にも対応する必要があり、地方自治体の
財政負担は大きい。家庭の経済環境による教育格差も大きい。所得水準の低い地域では、学習支
援や進学機会にも制約があり、教育機会の不平等が固定化しやすい。今回の抗議運動は、教育へ
の財政投下が学校現場の環境改善や格差縮小に十分につながっていないことが、不満の背景にあ
ると考えられる。
学生を中心に始まった抗議運動は、社会の幅広い層に広がっている。10 月17 日のデモには、主
要な労働組合の関係者、2018 年に大規模な抗議運動を展開した「黄色いベスト運動」の参加者、
年金生活者、農業従事者、環境団体、左派政党の関係者などが加わった。それぞれの要求は必ず

しも一致しないが、生計費負担の増加や公共サービスの質の低下、政府の経済・社会政策への不
満という点で共通している。
新型コロナ危機後の供給制約やロシアによるウクライナ侵攻後のエネルギー価格の高騰に端を
発した生計費危機の影響は今も続いている。物価上昇率がピークアウトした後も、伸び率が鈍化
しただけで、その後も物価は上がり続けている。そこにイラン情勢の緊迫化に伴う原油や天然ガ
ス価格の再高騰が加わり、家計負担が再び高まっている。特に低所得者層や若年層では、食料
品、燃料費、住居費などが支出全体に占める割合が高く、物価高の影響を受けやすい。世論は今
のところ抗議活動を概ね支持している。調査会社Elabeが10 月 16 日に公表した世論調査では、生
活費高騰に対する抗議運動に支持や共感を示した回答者は69％に達した。
今後の焦点は、抗議運動が持続的な社会運動に発展するかである。主要労働組合は11 月５日に
全国的なストライキ・デモを実施することを発表している。賃上げや公共サービスの充実、雇用

維持などを掲げ、政府・民間の労働者に幅広く参加を呼び掛けている。同日は国民議会（下院）
で 2027 年予算案の歳出部分が審議される予定の時期と重なる。主要労働組合の共同歩調が続き、

### Page 3

3/6 
抗議活動がさらに広がれば、歳出抑制策を巡って政府が野党と妥協する際の政治的な制約が強ま
る可能性がある。また、ストライキが長期化すれば、経済・社会活動の下押しを通じて、景気や
税収への悪影響が広がる恐れがある。

■政府予算案の国民負担の増加に強まる反発 
今回の大規模な抗議運動は、政府が財政再建を目指す重要な時期に発生した。ルコルニュ政権
が 10 月１日に議会へ提出した2027 年予算案では、2026 年に国内総生産（ＧＤＰ）比で 5.4％へ
膨らむ見通しの財政赤字を、2027 年に同5.0％に縮小することを目指している。そのために必要
とされる財政改善措置は約540 億ユーロに達し、このうち約430 億ユーロを新たな予算措置で確
保する計画だ。フランスは主要先進国の中でも税・社会保障負担が重く、さらなる増税による企
業の競争力低下を避けるため、政府案は歳出抑制に重点を置いている。公務員給与の算定基準の
凍結、一定額を超える年金給付の物価連動の停止、医療・社会保障支出の抑制、地方自治体への
歳出削減要求などが盛り込まれた。国防など一部の優先分野では歳出を拡大する一方、その他の
分野では歳出の伸びを抑制する。 
問題は、巨額の財政改善措置を講じても、財政赤字の改善幅がＧＤＰ比で僅か0.4 ポイントに
とどまることである。現行制度の下で見込まれる歳出の増加や金利上昇に伴う利払い費の拡大が
財政を圧迫しているためだ。財政・政治懸念の高まりによる金利上昇が、景気の冷え込みや利払
い費の更なる増加を招く恐れがあり、財政政策の自由度は一段と低下している。 
 今回の抗議運動は、こうした歳出抑制策への政治的な抵抗を強める可能性がある。政府が予算
成立に向けた野党の協力を取り付けたり、国民の不満を和らげたりするために、公共サービスや
社会保障関連の歳出削減を緩和すれば、十分な代替財源を確保できない限り、財政再建が遠退
く。他方で、財政再建を優先して厳しい歳出抑制策を維持すれば、抗議運動が長期化するリスク
がある。政府は財政規律の回復と社会的な合意形成の間で難しい判断を迫られている。 
 ルコルニュ政権にとって最大の難関は、国民議会で過半数の議席を確保していないことだ。
2027 年予算を通常の議会審議によって成立させるには、野党の一部から協力を得る必要がある。
予算成立の鍵を握るのは、穏健左派の社会党（ＰＳ）と極右政党・国民連合（ＲＮ）の対応であ
る。両党は政府予算案に反対する立場では共通するが、求める政策修正の方向性が異なる。社会
党は富裕層・大企業への増税や社会保障給付の維持を求める一方、国民連合は移民関連支出や欧
州連合（ＥＵ）への拠出金の削減、行政経費の削減などを通じた財政再建を主張している。企業
の税負担軽減や物価対策の強化も掲げているが、ＥＵ拠出金の削減などには制度上の制約もあ
り、財政改善計画の実現可能性は疑わしい。 
 社会党は前年度の予算審議で、年金改革の停止などの政策的譲歩を引き出し、他の野党が提出
した内閣不信任案への賛成を見送ることで、予算成立と政権延命に協力した。だが、今年度の予
算協議では、マクロン大統領の目玉政策であった年金改革停止のような大きな譲歩を勝ち取るこ
とが難しい。来春に大統領選挙を控えており、政府への協力に慎重になっている。一方、国民連
合のルペン氏は、予算不成立による混乱が長引くよりも、不完全でも予算を成立させる方が望ま

### Page 4

4/6 
しいとの認識を示している。10 月13 日には、社会党が提出した政府予算案を本格審議前に退ける
動議に国民連合が反対票を投じ、政府は極右政党の協力で最初の関門を乗り切った。 
野党の協力が得られない場合、政府が憲法で認められた特別な立法手続きを利用する可能性も
ある。憲法49 条３項に基づく手続きでは、予算案の議会採決を迂回できるが、その場合、議会は
内閣不信任案を提出でき、不信任案が否決されれば法案は採択されたものと見做され、可決され
れば内閣は総辞職する。また、憲法47 条では、議会が予算案の提出から70 日以内に審議を終え
なければ、政府はオルドナンス（行政命令）によって予算を執行できると定められている。この
手法は現在の政治体制（第五共和制）下で使われた前例がなく、実施すれば大きな政治的な反発
を招く恐れがある。年内の予算成立が難しい場合、最低限の税徴収や行政サービスを継続する特
別法を国民議会で可決する選択肢もある。こうした手段を用いれば、野党との全面的な合意がな
くても予算措置を実施できる余地はある。だが、49 条３項や特別法の手続きでも、野党の部分的
な協力が必要で、政策的な譲歩の過程で財政再建が遠退く可能性がある。 
今後の予算審議は、10 月 20 日に歳入部分、27 日に社会保障予算案、11 月 17 日に予算案全体の
採決が予定されている。ただし、審議の進展や採決の結果次第で、日程は変更される可能性があ
る。まずは歳入部分の審議で政府が社会党や国民連合からどの程度の協力を取り付けることがで
きるかが最初の重要な関門となろう。 
金融市場にとって重要なのは、予算が成立するかどうかだけではない。野党との妥協によって
歳出抑制が緩和される場合も、実効性のある代替財源を確保できれば、必ずしも財政赤字目標の
達成が難しくなるとは限らない。反対に、実現可能性が不透明な歳出削減や増税に依存すれば、
見かけ上の財政改善額が確保されても、財政運営に対する信頼は回復しにくい。予算を成立させ
るための妥協と、財政再建策の実効性をどう両立させるかが問われている。

■大統領選では購買力と治安が二つの争点に 
 今回の抗議運動は、2027 年春の大統領選挙にも影響を及ぼす可能性がある。学生運動に端を発
した抗議活動は、生活費の高騰や公共サービスの劣化に対する国民の不満を浮き彫りにした。一
方、10 月17 日のデモでは一部が暴動に発展し、商店の略奪や放火被害が広がったことを受け、治
安や公共秩序の維持も重要な政治課題となっている。大統領選では、購買力の改善や公共サービ
スの充実に加え、暴力行為への対応や警察権限のあり方を巡って、各候補者の政策上の違いが明
確になる可能性がある。 
 今回の抗議運動に積極的に関与しているのが、極左政党・不服従のフランス（ＬＦＩ）のメラ
ンション氏だ。同氏は学生による抗議活動をいち早く支持し、10 月 17 日のデモにも参加した。教
育や医療などの公共サービスへの支出拡大、富裕層への増税、年金制度の見直しなどを訴える一
方、警察による抗議活動への対応を批判している。ただし、一部の参加者による暴力行為を巡っ
ては、他の政治勢力からＬＦＩへの批判も強まっており、平和的な抗議への支持と公共秩序の維
持をどう両立させるかが問われている。 
これに対し、国民連合のルペン氏は、生活費負担の軽減を訴える一方、暴動を厳しく批判し、

### Page 5

5/6 
治安対策の強化を重視している。今回の抗議運動に対する共感は左派支持層だけでなく国民連合
の支持層にも広がっているが、学生運動そのものへの支持には大きな違いがある。国民連合にと
っても、購買力対策と治安維持をどう具体的な政策に結びつけるかが重要な論点となる。 
大統領選に向けた各政治勢力の再編も進んでいる。10 月 11 日には、欧州議会議員のグリュック
スマン氏が社会党を中心とする社会民主勢力の統一候補に選出され、メランション氏が率いる不
服従のフランスとの選挙協力に慎重な姿勢を示している。一方、中道勢力ではフィリップ元首相
やアタル元首相らの候補者調整がなお課題となっている。最近の世論調査では、ルペン氏が初回
投票で３割超の支持を集める一方、メランション氏、グリュックスマン氏、フィリップ氏が二番
手争いを繰り広げている。支持率は候補者の組み合わせによって異なり、今回の暴動後の変化も
現時点では十分確認できない。 
来春の大統領選が近づくことは、今後の予算協議を複雑にする。社会党は政府との妥協を通じ
て政策修正を実現することと、野党としての立場を維持することの間で判断を迫られる。一方、
国民連合も政府予算案への反対姿勢を示しながら、予算不成立による混乱をどこまで容認するか
という問題に直面している。各党の対応は、単なる予算案への賛否にとどまらず、財政責任と政
府への対抗姿勢をどのように両立させるかという問題でもある。 
さらに重要なのは、次期政権がどのような経済・財政政策を掲げる場合でも、厳しい財政制約
から逃れられないことである。公共サービスの拡充や購買力対策を進めるには財源が必要となる
一方、歳出削減や増税を進めれば社会的反発を招く可能性がある。大統領選では各候補者の政策
公約だけでなく、その財源や実行可能性が問われることになる。

■財政再建と社会的合意形成の両立が最大の課題 
今回の抗議運動は、2018 年の黄色いベスト運動と共通する特徴を持つ。特定の政策や生活上の
不満を出発点とする抗議が、政府の経済・社会政策全般への不信に結びつき、全国規模の運動へ
広がったことだ。ただし、燃料税引き上げへの反発から始まった黄色いベスト運動とは異なり、
今回は教育環境の改善を求める学生が運動の発端となった。若者の将来不安に、物価高や公共サ
ービスの劣化に対する幅広い層の不満が結びついた点に特徴がある。今後の焦点は、学生と労働
組合の連携がどこまで持続するか、11 月５日に予定される全国的なストライキ・デモがどの程度
の規模となるかである。 
抗議運動の長期化は、2027 年予算の成立を一段と難しくする可能性がある。政府は歳出抑制を
中心とする財政再建を目指しているが、野党の協力なしに予算を成立させることは難しい。社会
党は社会保障給付や公共サービスへの支出抑制に反対し、国民連合もＥＵの拠出金や移民関連支
出の削減を中心とした財政再建策を要求している。大規模な抗議活動が続けば、野党が政府に歳
出削減策の修正を求める圧力が強まろう。政府が野党の要求に応じれば、予算成立に近づく一
方、十分な代替財源を確保しない限り、財政再建が遠退く。他方、憲法49 条３項などを利用して
議会採決を回避しようとすれば、内閣不信任や更なる社会的反発を招く恐れがある。 
金融市場への波及にも注意が必要だ。フランスでは財政悪化と政治的不透明感を背景に国債が

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
6/6 
売られ、10 年物国債利回りは10 月中旬に５％近くまで上昇した。ドイツ国債との利回り格差（ス
プレッド）も一時150bpを超え、財政再建の実行可能性に対する市場の不安が高まっている。予算
協議の混乱や財政再建策の後退が現実味を増せば、財政運営への信認が損なわれ、国債利回りに
さらなる上昇圧力がかかる可能性がある。国債利回りの上昇は、新規発行や借り換えを通じて政
府の利払い費を押し上げ、財政再建を一段と難しくする。国債市場の不安定化が政府の財政運営
を制約し、それが政治的な不満や予算協議の難航につながるような、悪循環にも注意が必要だ。 
来春に大統領選挙を控えていることも、予算協議の先行きを複雑にしている。各党は政府の財
政運営への批判を強める一方、予算不成立による政治的混乱をどこまで容認するかという判断を
迫られる。選挙戦では、購買力の改善、公共サービスの充実、治安の回復などが重要な争点とな
り、各候補者が独自の政策を打ち出すとみられる。しかし、財政余力が乏しいなかでは、歳出拡
大や減税を掲げても、その財源をどう確保するかという問題を避けて通れない。各党が掲げる財
政再建策の実現可能性も、選挙後の政策運営を考えるうえで重要となる。 
 フランスでは財政赤字と公的債務が膨らみ、財政再建を先送りする余地は狭まっている。しか
し、社会保障や公共サービスへの歳出抑制を進めようとすれば国民の反発が強まり、議会での合
意形成が難しくなる。今回の大規模抗議運動は、財政再建の必要性が高まる一方で、その負担を
国民に求めるための政治的・社会的な合意形成が難しくなっているという、フランスが抱える構
造的な問題を改めて浮き彫りにした。来春の大統領選で誰が政権を担うことになっても、財政規
律の回復と国民生活の改善をいかに両立させるかという難題から逃れることはできない。

以 上

Document 477: フランス極右が財政健全化を掲げる狙い ～見え隠れする政治意図、問われる実現可能性～

Source PDF: all report pdf/フランス極右が財政健全化を掲げる狙い ～見え隠れする政治意図、問われる実現可能性～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-07

### Page 1

Europe Trends

2026 年 10 月７日

1/2 
フランス極右が財政健全化を掲げる狙い 
～見え隠れする政治意図、問われる実現可能性～ 
 経済調査部 首席エコノミスト  田中 理 
（℡：050-5474-7494）

（要旨） 
○ 政権獲得を目指すフランスの極右政党・国民連合（ＲＮ）は、2027 年大統領選挙を前に代替
予算案と中期財政計画を発表し、政権発足から 18 ヶ月以内に基礎的財政収支を均衡させ、
2030 年までに財政赤字の対ＧＤＰ比率を３％未満に縮小する計画を明らかにした。 企業減税
や年金負担の軽減を維持しながら、 移民への社会保障給付の抑制、 ＥＵ予算への拠出金削減、
行政機関の統廃合などの歳出削減を計画するが、その多くに法的・政治的な制約がある。 
○ 財政規律を前面に出す背景には、大統領選挙での勝利を睨み、産業界や金融市場の警戒を和
らげ、政権担当能力をアピールする狙いがある。仮に政権を獲得した場合、財政計画の実効
性が国債市場から厳しく問われ、政策の軌道修正を迫られる可能性がある。

2027 年春のフランス大統領選挙での勝利を目指す極右政党・国民連合（ＲＮ）は６日、政府の
2027 年予算案に対する代替予算案と、向こう５年間の中期財政計画を発表した。政権発足から18
ヶ月以内に基礎的財政収支を均衡させ、2030 年までに財政赤字の国内総生産（ＧＤＰ）比を３％
未満、2032 年には2.5％未満に縮小する。公的債務残高のＧＤＰ比を2027 年の 121％前後から、
2032 年には112％に低下させ、財布支出のＧＤＰ比を50％未満に抑える計画だ。2032 年までに
2026 年対比で1400 億ユーロ規模の財政赤字の削減を目指すとしている。さらに、財政赤字を債務
比率の安定に必要な水準に抑えるため、独自の財政ルール（黄金律）を憲法に盛り込み、政権獲
得後１年程度を目途に国民投票にかける考えも示した。 
かつて欧州連合（ＥＵ）の財政規律が国家主権を制約すると批判していたＲＮが、財政赤字の
削減や政府債務の安定を政権公約の柱に据えようとしているのは大きな変化と言える。そこには
大統領選挙を睨んでの政治的な意図が見え隠れする。ＲＮのルペン氏は各種の世論調査で初回投
票の支持率で他候補を圧倒的にリードしているが、決選投票での勝利を確実なものにするには、
ＲＮ政権誕生を警戒する有権者にも支持を広げる必要がある。フランスの財政リスクを巡って金
融市場の緊張が高まるなか、健全な財政運営をアピールすることで、政権担当能力への不安を払
拭する狙いもあるのだろう。 
ＲＮ案では、家計や企業への負担を軽減しながら、大規模な歳出削減を進める。年金の支給開

### Page 2

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
2/2 
始年齢の引き下げ、エネルギーや生活必需品の付加価値税（ＶＡＴ）の引き下げ、中小企業や設
備投資への税優遇などを進めるとしており、その財源として、ＥＵへの拠出金の削減、外国人向
けの社会保障給付や医療費の抑制、行政機関の整理や地方政府の効率化などを計画している。問
題はその実現可能性だ。例えば、ＥＵ予算への拠出金は、加盟国政府が自由に決められるもので
はなく、他の加盟国との合意が必要になる。防衛や競争力の強化などでＥＵの役割が拡大するな
か、今後検討が本格化するＥＵの2028～34 年の次期多年度財政枠組みは、史上最高水準の予算規
模となることが見込まれている。フランスが一方的に拠出金を削れば、ＥＵや他の加盟国との衝
突が避けられない。移民関連支出の削減にも限界がある。外国人への社会保障給付を制限する措
置は、フランスの憲法やＥＵ法との整合性が問われる可能性がある。また、行政機関の統廃合や
公務員の削減などは比較的実行しやすい政策と言えるが、想定する規模の財政節減効果が得られ
るかは不透明だ。実際に政権を担当すれば、政策の軌道修正を迫られる可能性が高い。 
ＲＮは近年、企業経営者や金融市場関係者との対話の機会を増やしてきた。今年４月には、Ｒ
Ｎの首相候補であるバルデラ党首とフランス最大の経営者団体の幹部との会談も行われた。ＲＮ
は、企業負担の軽減、規制緩和、行政事務の効率化など、財界が求めてきた政策を積極的に取り
入れている。今回の中期財政計画も、産業界や金融市場を強く意識した内容と言える。ただ、Ｒ
Ｎの経済・財政運営に対する警戒感が払拭されたわけではない。歳出削減が計画通りに進まなか
った場合、最終的に企業増税で穴埋めされるとの懸念もある、仮にＲＮが政権を獲得しても、Ｅ
Ｕ拠出金の削減が加盟国との交渉で難航し、移民関連支出の削減が司法判断などで制約される一
方、減税や年金の負担軽減措置だけが先行すれば、財政再建への信認は急速に失われかねない。 
ＲＮが掲げる財政規律への転換は、 「抗議政党」から「政権を担う責任政党」への脱皮を目指す
動きと位置づけられる。だが、財政再建の成否を左右するのは、支持層への負担増を避けなが
ら、歳出削減を実際に実行できるかだ。政権を獲得すれば、ＥＵとの交渉や司法上の制約、産業
界からの要求、そして国債市場からの圧力に直面する。選挙前に掲げた財政計画をどこまで現実
路線に修正できるかも、ＲＮの政権担当能力を測る重要な試金石となる。

以 上

事件  1
10/19 中國第三季 GDP 年增 4.5% 優於預期， 9 ⽉社零年增 1.1%
五⼤重點
1   中國國家統計局 10/19 公布，第三季 GDP 年增 4.5% ，⾼於市場預期 4.4% 與第⼆季的 4.3% ，季
增 1.2% ；前三季累計年增 4.6% ，第⼀季為 5.0% 。
2  9 ⽉社會消費品零售總額年增 1.1% ，⾼於預期 0.6% 與 8 ⽉的 0.4% ，其中汽⾞類年減 21.2% ，扣

除汽⾞後年增 3.9% 。

### Page 2

3   統計局說明，成⻑主要來⾃⽣產較快增⻑與外貿增勢良好，並坦⾔國內需求不⾜問題仍較突
出；前三季出⼝年增 16.4% ，⾼技術製造業增加值增 14.3% 。
4   前三季固定資產投資年減 7.2% ，與 1 ⾄ 8 ⽉持平，其中房地產開發投資年減 20.6% 、⺠間投資年
減 10.4% ，投資端仍在收縮。
5  9 ⽉規模以上⼯業增加值年增 5.5% ，符合預期，城鎮調查失業率由 5.3% 降⾄ 5.2% ；前三季 CPI
年增僅 0.9% ，需求端價格仍弱。
三⼤結論
1   第三季回升主要靠出⼝與⾼技術製造，投資與房地產仍深度收縮，屬於供給強、需求弱的結
構，不宜因單季 GDP 優於預期就提⾼中國股市總體權重。
2  9 ⽉社零回升但汽⾞與耐久財持續下滑，居⺠⾃主消費尚未全⾯修復；當社零、⺠間投資與房
地產資⾦連續兩季同步改善時，應再提⾼中國內需相關部位。
3   成⻑⾼度依賴外需，使中國經濟易受關稅與晶⽚價格影響，中美貿易休戰僅延⾄ 2027 年 1 ⽉ 10
⽇；中國資產維持選擇性配置，以具競爭⼒的新產業為主。

10/19 費城半導體指數漲 1.61% ，終結 2008 年來最⻑的⼋連跌
五⼤重點
1   費城半導體指數 10/19 收 11,316.34 點、漲 1.61% ，終結截⾄ 10/16 的⼋連跌；這段連跌為 2008
年以來最⻑， 10/7 ⾄ 10/16 累計跌 15.7% 。
2   那斯達克指數漲 1.11% 收 26,430.89 點，標普 500 指數漲 0.41% 、道瓊跌 0.07% ，但等權重標普
500 ETF 下跌 0.32% ，漲勢集中在少數⼤型科技股。
3   亞⾺遜漲 4.98% ，為 7 ⽉以來最⼤單⽇漲幅，公司同⽇推出讓 PyTorch 在⾃研 Trainium 晶⽚上執
⾏的免費⼯具；美光漲近 3% 。
4   反彈未擴及信⽤市場， CoreWeave 盤中⼀度漲逾 7% 後收跌 0.4% ，其 2032 年到期債券利差仍約
1,000 個基點，資料中⼼相關債券利差續處⾼檔。
5   同⽇ 10 年期美債殖利率上升約 6 個基點⾄ 5.15% ， VIX （波動率指數）下跌 7.75% ⾄ 18.44 ，股

市恐慌情緒降溫但利率壓⼒未解。
三⼤結論

### Page 3

1   半導體反彈由⼤型權值股帶動，等權重指數反⽽下跌，市場廣度仍窄，屬於⾼利率下的狹窄成
⻑⾏情；當 10 年期殖利率續升且市場廣度未改善時，應降低⾼ beta 半導體與記憶體部位。
2   半導體需求仍有實際出貨⽀撐，維持以訂單、⽑利與⾃由現⾦流篩選並分批配置；記憶體股反
彈須由報價、⻑約履約與存貨天數驗證，不宜追價。
3  AI 基礎設施的⾵險已轉向融資端， CoreWeave 債券利差約 1,000 個基點顯⽰信⽤市場仍要求⾼
補償；當資料中⼼信⽤利差持續擴⼤時，應先減少依賴外部融資的雲端與算⼒租賃標的。

10/19 法國抗議潮擴⼤， 10 年期公債殖利率單⽇升 13.1 個基點⾄ 4.936%
五⼤重點
1   法國 10 年期公債殖利率 10/19 收 4.936% ，單⽇上升 13.1 個基點，為 2025 年 3 ⽉以來最⼤單⽇升
幅；法德 10 年期利差擴⼤⾄約 147 個基點，為兩週⾼點。
2   抗議訴求集中於⽣活成本上漲與公共服務縮減， 10/17 全法逾 2,000 ⼈被捕；總理勒科爾尼

10/19 宣布啟動⼤規模司法⾏動，巴黎與北部省部分市鎮實施宵禁。
3   壓⼒外溢⾄其他⾼負債國，義⼤利 10 年期殖利率升 7.8 個基點⾄ 4.566% ；法國 CAC 40 指數跌
1.1% ，法國巴黎銀⾏與興業銀⾏分別跌 2.5% 與 3.4% 。
4   法國主權 CDS （信⽤違約交換）上升 5 個基點⾄約 82 個基點； CAC 40 相對德國 DAX 的估值折
價擴⼤⾄逾 13% ，為 2009 年初以來最⼤。
5   穆迪預定 10/23 檢視法國主權評級，國會同時審議預算； 10 ⽉初法國 10 年期殖利率曾觸及
4.99% ，為 2002 年以來最⾼。
三⼤結論
1   法國利差擴⼤源⾃財政整頓遭遇社會阻⼒，屬法國個案的財政與政治⾵險，不宜直接外推為歐
元區普遍違約⾵險；配置上應降低法國主權存續期間與歐元集中曝險。
2   當 10/23 穆迪下調評級或預算審議受阻、法德利差重回 150 個基點以上時，應進⼀步減少法國
公債與法國銀⾏股部位，改以跨國低負債、⾼現⾦流企業分散歐洲曝險。

3   法國動盪使歐元承壓，歐元兌美元 10/19 收 1.1212 ；歐元雖有政策利差⽀撐，但能源成本與主
權分化限制升值品質，歐元部位宜控制集中度並等待預算與通膨數據改善。
